Appendix A: Cilium CLI Command Reference
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  CILIUM CLI  (github.com/cilium/cilium-cli binary)           │
         │    install, uninstall, upgrade, status, connectivity         │
         │    test, clustermesh *, hubble ui/enable — Ch5, Ch17         │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium-dbg  (compiled into the Agent image; run             │
         │    via kubectl exec ds/cilium -- cilium-dbg ...)             │
         │    endpoint, policy, monitor, config, bpf, encrypt,          │
         │    identity, bugtool — Ch6, Ch7.8, Ch9                       │
         │                                                              │
         │  hubble  (separate binary, talks to Hubble Relay)            │
         │    observe, status, list nodes — Ch13, Ch15                  │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ * clustermesh is a Cilium CLI subcommand, not             │
         │    cilium-dbg — it drives cluster-to-cluster setup,          │
         │    not one Agent's own local state (see A.8).                │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 Ch6.4 already shortens "cilium-dbg config" to             │
         │    "cilium config" for readability — same binary,            │
         │    same shorthand applies to every entry below.              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.1  Cluster Management Commands
         - cilium install / uninstall / upgrade / status
         - cilium install wraps Helm and bootstraps the whole
           release in one shot — Ch5.3's annotated walkthrough
         - cilium upgrade re-renders the SAME Helm release — the
           ONLY supported way to change a durable value (Ch6.4)

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  cilium install [--set key=val ...] [--context <c>]          │
         │    bootstraps Cilium end-to-end — wraps Helm (Ch5.3)         │
         │                                                              │
         │    --set kubeProxyReplacement=true                           │
         │      → replace kube-proxy                                    │
         │    --set hubble.enabled=true                                 │
         │      → turn on Hubble (CLI+Relay+UI)                         │
         │    --set encryption.enabled=true                             │
         │      → WireGuard on (Ch16)                                   │
         │    --context <name>                                          │
         │      → target one kubeconfig context                         │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium status [--wait] [--context <ctx>]                    │
         │    health dashboard: Agent/Operator/Hubble/Mesh (6.1)        │
         │    --wait                                                    │
         │      → block until every pod is Ready (Ch5.4)                │
         │    --context <name>                                          │
         │      → check one cluster's health (Ch17)                     │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium upgrade --set key=value [--reuse-values]             │
         │    re-renders the SAME Helm release — the ONLY               │
         │    supported way to change a value durably (Ch6.4)           │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium uninstall [--context <name>]                         │
         │    removes the Cilium Helm release + CRDs entirely           │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 install/upgrade/uninstall/status all take                 │
         │    --context — the same cluster-targeting flag               │
         │    Ch17's Cluster Mesh workflow relies on (A.8).             │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.2  Endpoint Commands
         - cilium endpoint list / inspect / disconnect
         - real cilium-dbg subcommands: list, get <id>, config,
           labels, disconnect (Ch7.8's live enforcement check)
         - anihpj's own endpoint list output — Ch7.8

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  cilium endpoint list  (or cilium-dbg endpoint list)         │
         │    every pod THIS Agent manages, one row each — 7.8          │
         │                                                              │
         │    ENDPOINT  POLICY(ingress)  POLICY(egress)  LABELS         │
         │    1928      Disabled         Disabled        postgres       │
         │    (real output also shows IDENTITY, IPv4, STATUS —          │
         │    Ch7.8's live enforcement-mode check)                      │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium endpoint get <id>                                    │
         │    one endpoint's full record, by ID                         │
         │    -o json | yaml | jsonpath='{}'                            │
         │      → pick the output shape                                 │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium endpoint inspect                                     │
         │    deep dive into ONE pod's live policy state (6.2)          │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium endpoint disconnect <id>                             │
         │    disconnects that endpoint from the network                │
         │                                                              │
         │  ⚠️  endpoint IDs are ephemeral + node-local — a             │
         │    restart/reschedule assigns a NEW one (Ch9's own           │
         │    Mistake 3). Re-run endpoint list before filtering.        │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.3  Policy Commands
         - cilium policy get / import / delete / validate
         - these read/edit the AGENT's own local policy cache —
           day-to-day CNP/CCNP changes go through kubectl
           apply/delete -f instead (Ch7, Ch8's real YAML)

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  cilium policy get [-o json|yaml|jsonpath='{}']              │
         │    print every policy rule this Agent has loaded             │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium policy import <path>                                 │
         │    load a policy file straight into the Agent —              │
         │    bypasses the CNP/CCNP CRDs Ch7-8 apply via                │
         │    kubectl (rarely needed once you're on K8s CRDs)           │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium policy delete <labels>                               │
         │    remove rules matching a label selector from the           │
         │    Agent's local policy repository                           │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium policy validate <path>                               │
         │    checks a policy file's SYNTAX only — does NOT             │
         │    apply it (a typo'd label VALUE still passes this;         │
         │    only kubectl apply + Hubble catch that — Ch15)            │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 every CNP/CCNP in Ch7-8 is a real k8s object —            │
         │    kubectl apply -f is the actual workflow; get/             │
         │    import/delete/validate are the Agent-local                │
         │    equivalents behind that same policy repository.           │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.4  Hubble Commands
         - hubble observe / status / list
         - hubble observe's real flags — Ch13.3, Ch15.5's own
           quick-reference table (reused verbatim below)
         - launching the UI is a Cilium CLI command, cilium
           hubble ui (Ch13.4) — not the hubble binary itself

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  hubble observe [flags]                                      │
         │    ALL flows (firehose) unless filtered — Ch13.3             │
         │                                                              │
         │    --namespace anihpj                                        │
         │      → scope to our namespace                                │
         │    --to-namespace / --from-namespace                         │
         │      → one-direction scoping, IN vs OUT of anihpj            │
         │    --pod / --to-pod / --from-pod  (ns/name form!)            │
         │      → ONE exact pod, e.g. anihpj/postgres-0                 │
         │    --verdict V                                               │
         │      → FORWARDED, DROPPED, AUDIT, or ERROR                   │
         │    --protocol P                                              │
         │      → tcp, udp, http, dns, etc.                             │
         │    --http-status / --http-method                             │
         │      → find 500s / POSTs (Ch15.1)                            │
         │    --last N                                                  │
         │      → N most recent buffered flows                          │
         │    --follow  (-f)                                            │
         │      → live-tail, like tail -f (Ch15.2)                      │
         │    -o json                                                   │
         │      → machine-readable, pipe to jq (15.2, 15.4)             │
         │                                                              │
         │  hubble status                                               │
         │    Healthcheck (via localhost:4245): Ok                      │
         │    Current/Max Flows: 16384/16384 (100.00%)                  │
         │    Flows/s: 24.89   Connected Nodes: 3/3                     │
         │                                                              │
         │  hubble list nodes                                           │
         │    NAME             STATUS     FLOWS/S  CURR/MAX             │
         │    cluster/node-w1  Connected  51.37    5108/9840            │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium hubble ui   (Cilium CLI, not the hubble bin)         │
         │    port-forwards hubble-ui locally (Ch13.4) —                │
         │    http://localhost:12000, blocks the terminal               │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.5  Connectivity Testing
         - cilium connectivity test
         - runs 21+ checks: pod-to-pod, service, DNS, policy,
           encryption, Hubble (Ch5.4's own test-category table)
         - --multi-cluster validates a live Cluster Mesh link
           end-to-end, not just clustermesh status (Ch17.2)

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  cilium connectivity test [flags]                            │
         │    runs 21+ checks (Ch5.4): pod-to-pod, services,            │
         │    DNS, network policy, encryption, Hubble, perf             │
         │                                                              │
         │    --context <name>                                          │
         │      → cluster to test FROM                                  │
         │    --multi-cluster <ctx>                                     │
         │      → also test TO a connected cluster — real               │
         │      Cluster Mesh reachability (Ch17.2 Step 5)               │
         │    --test <regex>                                            │
         │      → run only matching tests; prefix "!" to SKIP           │
         │    --test-concurrency N                                      │
         │      → namespaces tested in parallel                         │
         │                                                              │
         │  $ cilium connectivity test --context us-east \              │
         │    --multi-cluster eu-west                                   │
         │    # Ch17.2 Step 5 — confirms us-east can ACTUALLY           │
         │    # reach eu-west, not just that clustermesh                │
         │    # status reports "OK"                                     │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 run this after ANY Cilium change (Ch5's own               │
         │    Mistake 3) — a green cilium status never proves           │
         │    traffic actually flows end-to-end.                        │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.6  Service & Load Balancing Commands
         - cilium service list
         - cilium bpf lb list — Ch6.2's low-level eBPF view
         - Ch12's Mistake 3 fix: map a Service's external IP
           to its real backend pod IPs

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  cilium service list  (or cilium-dbg service list)           │
         │    every K8s Service via Cilium's eBPF LB —                  │
         │    ClusterIP/NodePort/LB mapped to real backends             │
         │                                                              │
         │    ID   FRONTEND            TYPE        BACKENDS             │
         │    12   10.96.0.55:5432     ClusterIP   2                    │
         │    (confirms a Service IP actually resolves to the           │
         │    anihpj/postgres-0 pod IP you expect — Ch12)               │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium-dbg bpf lb list                                      │
         │    the RAW eBPF load-balancer map entries UNDER              │
         │    service list — one line per service/backend               │
         │    tuple, keyed by IP:port                                   │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 service list = the friendly view. bpf lb list =           │
         │    the literal kernel map it's built from — reach            │
         │    for it only once service list itself looks wrong.         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.7  Encryption Commands
         - cilium encrypt status
         - cilium-dbg encrypt status/flush — real subcommands,
           plus Ch16.3's cilium status | grep Encryption check

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  cilium encrypt status  (or cilium-dbg encrypt status)       │
         │    "Display the current encryption state" —                  │
         │    WireGuard or IPsec, per Ch16.2's chosen mode              │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium encrypt flush  (or cilium-dbg encrypt flush)         │
         │    flushes IPsec's XFRM state — forces fresh key/            │
         │    state negotiation (IPsec-specific — Ch16.2)               │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium status | grep Encryption                             │
         │    the quick health-dashboard check from Ch16.3 —            │
         │    confirms "Encryption: OK (WireGuard)" at a glance         │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 there's no PER-POD encryption flag to query               │
         │    (Ch16's Mistake 1) — every command here reports           │
         │    NODE-PAIR tunnel state, never one pod's status.           │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.8  Cluster Mesh Commands
         - cilium clustermesh status / connect / disconnect
         - the real enable → status --wait → connect →
           status --wait → connectivity test workflow (Ch17.2)

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  cilium clustermesh enable --context <ctx>                   │
         │    deploys clustermesh-apiserver + exchanges mTLS            │
         │    certs — run on BOTH clusters (Ch17.2 Step 2)              │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium clustermesh status --context <ctx> --wait            │
         │    blocks until that cluster's API server is ready           │
         │    (Step 3) — re-run after connect to verify (Step 5)        │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium clustermesh connect --context <ctx> \                │
         │    --destination-context <ctx2>                              │
         │    peers two clusters — ONE direction only; Cilium           │
         │    auto-builds the reverse link (Ch17.2's own note)          │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium clustermesh disconnect --context <ctx> \             │
         │    --destination-context <ctx2>                              │
         │    tears down that peering, both directions                  │
         │                                                              │
         │  $ cilium clustermesh connect --context us-east \            │
         │    --destination-context eu-west                             │
         │    # Ch17.2 Step 4 — run from us-east ONLY;                  │
         │    # repeating it from eu-west is redundant,                 │
         │    # not wrong                                               │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 4 silent failure points if skipped (Ch17.4):              │
         │    unique cluster ID/name, non-overlapping Pod               │
         │    CIDRs, matching datapath mode, a reachable                │
         │    clustermesh-apiserver.                                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    A.9  Debugging Commands
         - cilium monitor / metrics list / bugtool
         - cilium-dbg config — Ch6.4's real, full form
         - cilium-dbg bugtool — Ch6.2's support-tarball tool

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  cilium monitor  (or cilium-dbg monitor)                     │
         │    live packet-level view — DROP/FORWARD/AUDIT per           │
         │    flow, on THIS node ONLY (Ch9's Mistake 1)                 │
         │                                                              │
         │    --type drop                                               │
         │      → only dropped packets                                  │
         │    --type policy-verdict                                     │
         │      → only policy decisions                                 │
         │    --from / --to <endpoint-id>                               │
         │      → one side of a flow                                    │
         │    --related-to <endpoint-id>                                │
         │      → either side matches that EP                           │
         │    -v, -vv                                                   │
         │      → more verbose output                                   │
         │    -j, --json                                                │
         │      → machine-readable JSON                                 │
         │                                                              │
         │  $ cilium monitor --type policy-verdict                      │
         │    action allow, match L3-L4 → a real rule matched           │
         │    action deny,  match none  → NO rule allows this           │
         │    (Ch9.2's own annotated output: read BOTH fields)          │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium-dbg config [-a|--all]                                │
         │    bare: the small LIVE-toggleable debug flag set            │
         │    -a: full dump, incl. flags fixed at install (6.4)         │
         │    full form: kubectl -n kube-system exec ds/cilium          │
         │    -- cilium-dbg config                                      │
         │                                                              │
         │  cilium-dbg bugtool  (or cilium debuginfo, quicker)          │
         │    generates a full support tarball — logs, BPF              │
         │    maps, policy state — for a real bug report (6.2)          │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 monitor is scoped to ONE node; for cluster-wide           │
         │    flow visibility, reach for Hubble instead (Ch9's          │
         │    own Mistake 1 fix — see A.4 above).                       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Putting It All Together — Quick Command Lookup by Task
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
         - One cheat sheet mapping a real question straight to
           the exact command to run — every row cross-
           references the fuller entry back in A.1-A.9 above

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  TASK                    RUN THIS                            │
         │  ──────────────────────  ────────────────────────────────────│
         │  Is Cilium healthy?      cilium status --wait                │
         │  Why's my pod blocked?   cilium monitor -t policy-verdict    │
         │  See live traffic now?   hubble observe --follow             │
         │  Who's flooding a Svc?   hubble observe -o json | jq         │
         │  Is my policy YAML ok?   cilium policy validate <path>       │
         │  Is traffic encrypted?   cilium encrypt status               │
         │  Cluster Mesh linked?    cilium clustermesh status --wait    │
         │  Did my change break                                         │
         │    anything?             cilium connectivity test            │
         │  What pod backs this                                         │
         │    Service IP?           cilium service list                 │
         │  This pod's endpoint                                         │
         │    ID right now?         cilium endpoint list                │
         │  Need a full bug-report                                      │
         │    bundle?               cilium-dbg bugtool                  │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 A.1-A.9 above give the full flag reference for            │
         │    every command in this table — this page is only           │
         │    the fast index BACK into those sections.                  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Appendix B: Cilium YAML Templates
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    B.1  CiliumNetworkPolicy — Basic allow
         - Scenario: only anihpj backend pods may reach postgres:5432
         - Copy-paste ready — swap metadata.name/namespace and the two matchLabels blocks for your own app
         - Full walkthrough of this exact pattern: Ch7.3
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: cilium.io/v2                                    │
         │  kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy                                   │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: allow-anihpj-to-postgres                            │
         │    namespace: anihpj        # CNP is namespace-scoped        │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    description: "anihpj backend may reach postgres:5432"     │
         │    endpointSelector:        # WHICH pods this rule protects  │
         │      matchLabels:                                            │
         │        app: postgres          # target = the PostgreSQL pods │
         │        component: database                                   │
         │    ingress:                 # allow-list for INCOMING traffic│
         │    - fromEndpoints:          # WHO may send traffic in       │
         │      - matchLabels:                                          │
         │          app: anihpj           # source = anihpj backend pods│
         │          component: backend                                  │
         │      toPorts:                # ON WHICH port(s)              │
         │      - ports:                                                │
         │        - port: "5432"          # PostgreSQL's TCP port       │
         │          protocol: TCP                                       │
         │                                                              │
         │  # Everything NOT explicitly allowed above is denied the     │
         │  # instant this rule selects the postgres pods (Ch7.3).      │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.2  CiliumNetworkPolicy — L7 HTTP rules
         - Scenario: public GET on jobpost's read endpoint; POST (create) restricted to the internal network
         - L7 HTTP awareness (method + path) is Cilium-only — plain K8s NetPol can't do this (Ch7.2)
         - Full walkthrough of this exact pattern: Ch7.4
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: cilium.io/v2                                    │
         │  kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy                                   │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: anihpj-jobpost-l7-http                              │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                         │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    description: "jobpost API: public reads, internal writes" │
         │    endpointSelector:                                         │
         │      matchLabels:                                            │
         │        app: anihpj           # target = the jobpost API pods │
         │    ingress:                                                  │
         │    # RULE 1: ANYONE may GET the public job listings          │
         │    - fromEndpoints:                                          │
         │      - {}                     # "{}" = any source, in or out │
         │      toPorts:                                                │
         │      - ports:                                                │
         │        - port: "8000"                                        │
         │          protocol: TCP                                       │
         │        rules:                                                │
         │          http:                                               │
         │          - method: "GET"       # ⬅ L7! Method-aware match    │
         │            path: "/jobs/"      # read-only listing endpoint  │
         │    # RULE 2: only INTERNAL callers may POST a new job        │
         │    - fromCIDR:                                               │
         │      - "10.0.0.0/8"           # internal network only        │
         │      toPorts:                                                │
         │      - ports:                                                │
         │        - port: "8000"                                        │
         │          protocol: TCP                                       │
         │        rules:                                                │
         │          http:                                               │
         │          - method: "POST"                                    │
         │            path: "/jobs/"      # create-job endpoint         │
         │                                                              │
         │  # A POST to /jobs/ from OUTSIDE 10.0.0.0/8 is DROPPED —     │
         │  # no rule above allows it (same pattern as Ch7.4).          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.3  CiliumNetworkPolicy — DNS-based rules
         - Scenario: anihpj may resolve AND call api.stripe.com — nothing else, in or out
         - Pairs a DNS-name allowlist (toPorts.rules.dns) with a toFQDNs egress rule — both used together
         - Full walkthrough of this exact pattern: Ch7.5 / Ch8.2
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: cilium.io/v2                                    │
         │  kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy                                   │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: anihpj-allow-stripe-fqdn                            │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                         │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    description: "anihpj may resolve + call api.stripe.com"   │
         │    endpointSelector:                                         │
         │      matchLabels:                                            │
         │        app: anihpj                                           │
         │    egress:                                                   │
         │    # RULE 1: allow DNS lookups to CoreDNS, but only let      │
         │    # it resolve this ONE allow-listed name                   │
         │    - toEndpoints:                                            │
         │      - matchLabels:                                          │
         │          k8s:io.kubernetes.pod.namespace: kube-system        │
         │          k8s-app: kube-dns                                   │
         │      toPorts:                                                │
         │      - ports:                                                │
         │        - port: "53"                                          │
         │          protocol: ANY                                       │
         │        rules:                                                │
         │          dns:                                                │
         │          - matchName: "api.stripe.com"  # exact FQDN only    │
         │    # RULE 2: once resolved, only THOSE IPs may be dialed,    │
         │    # and only on 443                                         │
         │    - toFQDNs:                                                │
         │      - matchName: "api.stripe.com"                           │
         │      toPorts:                                                │
         │      - ports:                                                │
         │        - port: "443"                                         │
         │          protocol: TCP                                       │
         │                                                              │
         │  # toPorts.rules.dns restricts WHICH NAMES may be resolved;  │
         │  # toFQDNs restricts WHICH RESOLVED IPs egress may reach.    │
         │  # Use both together — that's what actually stops DNS-       │
         │  # tunneled exfiltration (Ch7.5 / Ch8.2).                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.4  CiliumNetworkPolicy — Default deny all
         - Generic, drop-in "zero trust" starting point for ANY namespace — not anihpj-specific
         - An empty rule ("- {}") still counts as a rule — it just matches nothing, so everything drops
         - Concept + rollout strategy: Ch7.7
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: cilium.io/v2                                    │
         │  kind: CiliumNetworkPolicy                                   │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: default-deny-all                                    │
         │    namespace: <NAMESPACE>   # e.g. anihpj — any ns works     │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    description: "Zero-trust baseline: deny all in + out"     │
         │    endpointSelector: {}     # "{}" = every pod in this ns    │
         │    ingress:                                                  │
         │    - {}                     # empty rule = allow NOTHING in  │
         │    egress:                                                   │
         │    - {}                     # empty rule = allow NOTHING out │
         │                                                              │
         │  # A rule list containing ONLY "- {}" still counts as a      │
         │  # rule, so the endpoint starts ENFORCING that direction —   │
         │  # but it matches zero traffic, so everything is denied.     │
         │  # This is Cilium's documented default-deny-all idiom,       │
         │  # true across versions (incl. this guide's v1.16.0 ref).    │
         │  #                                                           │
         │  # Cilium v1.16 also ships a newer spec.enableDefaultDeny:   │
         │  # {ingress: true, egress: true} field meant to state the    │
         │  # same intent more explicitly — check your exact patch      │
         │  # version's docs before relying on it alone (no rules).     │
         │  #                                                           │
         │  # Apply a DNS-allow CNP (B.3 pattern) TOO, or pods can't    │
         │  # even resolve hostnames once this is enforced (Ch7         │
         │  # Common Pitfall #1).                                       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.5  CiliumClusterwideNetworkPolicy — Monitoring access
         - Scenario: Prometheus (monitoring ns) may GET /metrics on 9090 from EVERY pod, cluster-wide
         - Cluster-scoped: a CCNP NEVER has a metadata.namespace field
         - Full walkthrough of this exact pattern: Ch8.2 (CCNP Policy 2)
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: cilium.io/v2                                    │
         │  kind: CiliumClusterwideNetworkPolicy                        │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: allow-prometheus-scrape-all-namespaces              │
         │    # no 'namespace:' field — CCNP is cluster-scoped!         │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    description: "Prometheus may GET /metrics, cluster-wide"  │
         │    endpointSelector: {}   # target = ALL pods, ALL namespaces│
         │    ingress:                                                  │
         │    - fromEndpoints:                                          │
         │      - matchLabels:                                          │
         │          k8s:io.kubernetes.pod.namespace: monitoring         │
         │          app.kubernetes.io/name: prometheus                  │
         │      toPorts:                                                │
         │      - ports:                                                │
         │        - port: "9090"         # anihpj metrics port          │
         │          protocol: TCP                                       │
         │        rules:                                                │
         │          http:                                               │
         │          - method: "GET"       # L7! Scraping can't POST/    │
         │            path: "/metrics"    #     DELETE/PUT anything     │
         │                                                              │
         │  # Combine with a CNP (B.2): Prometheus gets GET /metrics    │
         │  # ONLY — it still can't reach /admin/ on jobpost (Ch8.2).   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.6  CiliumBGPPeeringPolicy
         - Peers Cilium (AS 64513) with a physical ToR router (AS 64512) and announces this node's Pod CIDR
         - This guide's v1.16.0 reference version still fully supports this single-CRD model
         - See the in-template VERSION NOTE before reusing this on a newer Cilium cluster
         - Full walkthrough incl. the newer 4-CRD model: Ch12.2
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: "cilium.io/v2alpha1"                            │
         │  kind: CiliumBGPPeeringPolicy                                │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: anihpj-rack0-bgp                                    │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    nodeSelector:            # which NODES run this policy    │
         │      matchLabels:                                            │
         │        rack: rack0                                           │
         │    virtualRouters:          # one Cilium-side BGP router     │
         │    - localASN: 64513         # Cilium's own AS number        │
         │      exportPodCIDR: true     # announce this node's Pod CIDR │
         │      neighbors:              # the physical router to peer   │
         │      - peerAddress: "10.0.0.1/32"  # ToR router's IP         │
         │        peerASN: 64512          # ToR router's AS number      │
         │      serviceSelector:        # WHICH Services get their IP   │
         │        matchExpressions:     #  advertised via BGP           │
         │        - key: somekey         # "match every Service" idiom: │
         │          operator: NotIn      #  a key that never exists     │
         │          values: ["never-used-value"]                        │
         │                                                              │
         │  # VERSION NOTE (see Ch12.2 in full): this guide's ref.      │
         │  # version (v1.16.0) still fully supports this single-CRD    │
         │  # CiliumBGPPeeringPolicy. It was marked DEPRECATED in       │
         │  # Cilium 1.18 and REMOVED in 1.19, replaced by 4 CRDs:      │
         │  # CiliumBGPClusterConfig / CiliumBGPPeerConfig /            │
         │  # CiliumBGPAdvertisement / CiliumBGPNodeConfigOverride.     │
         │  # Copying this onto a newer Cilium? Migrate first.          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.7  CiliumEgressGatewayPolicy
         - Scenario: anihpj calling Stripe's API must always appear from ONE fixed, allowlistable IP
         - Cluster-scoped (no metadata.namespace); selectors use PLAIN k8s labels — not CNP's "k8s:"-prefix
         - Full walkthrough incl. HA egressGateways + the Cluster Mesh caveat: Ch18.2-18.3
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: cilium.io/v2                                    │
         │  kind: CiliumEgressGatewayPolicy                             │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: anihpj-stripe-egress  # cluster-scoped, no ns field │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    selectors:                                                │
         │    - podSelector:                                            │
         │        matchLabels:            # PLAIN k8s labels — no "k8s:"│
         │          app: anihpj             # prefix (unlike CNP above!)│
         │          io.kubernetes.pod.namespace: anihpj                 │
         │    destinationCIDRs:                                         │
         │    - "203.0.113.0/24"        # e.g. Stripe's published range │
         │    excludedCIDRs:              # OPTIONAL carve-out, SNAT'd  │
         │    - "203.0.113.128/25"       # normally (skips the gateway) │
         │    egressGateway:                                            │
         │      nodeSelector:             # which NODE is the gateway   │
         │        matchLabels:                                          │
         │          egress-gateway: "true"                              │
         │      egressIP: "198.51.100.10"  # fixed IP the partner allows│
         │      # interface: eth1  <- alt. to egressIP, NEVER both      │
         │      #                     set at the same time              │
         │                                                              │
         │  # Needs 3 install-time Helm prereqs (Ch18.2): BPF           │
         │  # masquerade, kube-proxy replacement, and identity          │
         │  # allocation mode = kubernetes (not kvstore).               │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    B.8  CiliumLocalRedirectPolicy
         - Scenario: redirect kube-dns Service traffic to a NodeLocal DNSCache pod on the SAME node
         - ⚠️  NOT covered elsewhere in this guide — verified against upstream Cilium docs/examples instead
         - Requires the Helm flag --set localRedirectPolicy=true at install time
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: cilium.io/v2                                    │
         │  kind: CiliumLocalRedirectPolicy                             │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: anihpj-nodelocal-dns-cache                          │
         │    namespace: kube-system                                    │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    redirectFrontend:        # traffic to INTERCEPT           │
         │      serviceMatcher:         # match an existing Service...  │
         │        serviceName: kube-dns                                 │
         │        namespace: kube-system                                │
         │        # (alternative: addressMatcher: {ip, toPorts} to      │
         │        #  match a raw destination IP instead of a Service —  │
         │        #  the two are mutually exclusive, pick ONE)          │
         │    redirectBackend:         # where to send it INSTEAD       │
         │      localEndpointSelector:                                  │
         │        matchLabels:                                          │
         │          k8s-app: node-local-dns  # THIS node's own cache    │
         │      toPorts:                                                │
         │      - port: "53"                                            │
         │        protocol: UDP                                         │
         │      - port: "53"                                            │
         │        protocol: TCP                                         │
         │                                                              │
         │  # Redirects kube-dns Service traffic to the node-local      │
         │  # DNS cache pod running on the SAME node — the classic      │
         │  # NodeLocal DNSCache pattern. Requires the Helm flag        │
         │  # --set localRedirectPolicy=true at install time.           │
         │  #                                                           │
         │  # NOT covered elsewhere in this guide — verified against    │
         │  # upstream Cilium docs/examples (cilium/cilium repo), not   │
         │  # this guide's other chapters. Optional field               │
         │  # skipRedirectFromBackend: true (kernel >= 5.12) lets the   │
         │  # node-local-dns pod ITSELF bypass the redirect for its     │
         │  # own upstream queries.                                     │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Appendix C: Common Errors and Solutions
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    C.1  "Cilium agent not ready" errors
    C.2  "Policy not working" troubleshooting
    C.3  Hubble connectivity issues
    C.4  Cluster Mesh connection failures
    C.5  Encryption configuration errors

         This appendix is a fast, symptom-first lookup, not a replacement for
         the full debugging methodology already taught in Ch5/6 (install), Ch9
         (policy debugging), Ch13/15 (Hubble), Ch16 (encryption), and Ch17
         (Cluster Mesh). Each row below cross-references the chapter section
         where the full walkthrough lives. If you already know which chapter's
         problem you have, jump straight to the matching table. If you don't,
         start with the triage flowchart immediately below.

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  🔴 "My Cilium thing doesn't work — where do I start?"        │
         │       │                                                      │
         │       ▼                                                      │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────┐                                 │
         │  │ cilium status: are ALL  │──NO──▶ Agent CrashLoop /        │
         │  │ Cilium pods Ready?      │        NotReady / stuck?        │
         │  └────────────┬────────────┘        Go to C.1                │
         │               │ YES (agent itself is healthy)                │
         │               ▼                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────┐                                 │
         │  │ Does this involve TWO   │──YES─▶ Cluster Mesh won't       │
         │  │ OR MORE clusters?       │        connect / stay synced?   │
         │  └────────────┬────────────┘        Go to C.4                │
         │               │ NO (one cluster)                             │
         │               ▼                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────┐                                 │
         │  │ Is the question "IS     │──YES─▶ WireGuard/IPSec not      │
         │  │ traffic encrypted?"     │        showing OK, or mixed     │
         │  └────────────┬────────────┘        modes? Go to C.5         │
         │               │ NO                                           │
         │               ▼                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────┐                                 │
         │  │ Can you even SEE the    │──NO──▶ hubble ui/observe        │
         │  │ flow in Hubble at all?  │        blank, Relay down, or    │
         │  └────────────┬────────────┘        ring buffer emptied?     │
         │               │ YES (flow is visible, verdict is the issue)  │
         │               ▼                                    Go to C.3 │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────┐                                 │
         │  │ Traffic is ALLOWED or   │──▶ Selector / namespace /       │
         │  │ DENIED when it          │    DNS-egress / audit-mode bug? │
         │  │ shouldn't be            │    Go to C.2                    │
         │  └─────────────────────────┘                                 │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 Work top to bottom — an agent that isn't Ready explains   │
         │     EVERY downstream symptom below it, so C.1 always comes   │
         │     first. Only chase C.2-C.5 once cilium status is clean.   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    C.1  "Cilium agent not ready" errors
         - Covers Ch5's install-time failure modes: bad kernel, leftover CNI,
           broken RBAC, and undersized nodes -- if cilium status won't go green,
           nothing in C.2-C.5 can be trusted either, so always fix this section
           first (see the triage flowchart above).
         - Full walkthroughs: Ch5.1 (prerequisites), Ch5.5 (CNI migration),
           and Ch6.1 (reading cilium status).

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SYMPTOM/ERROR        ROOT CAUSE          SOLUTION           │
         │  ───────────────────  ──────────────────  ─────────────────  │
         │  cilium-xxxxx pods    Node kernel below   Upgrade kernel to  │
         │  CrashLoopBackOff;    5.10 -- missing     5.10+ (5.15/6.1+   │
         │  log: "kernel         eBPF features       ideal, Ch5.1);     │
         │  version: NOT OK"     Ch5.1 requires      confirm via uname  │
         │                                           -r, reinstall      │
         │  Pods stuck           Old CNI             Run Ch5.5's        │
         │  ContainerCreating;   (Calico/Flannel)    4-phase            │
         │  kubelet condition    conflist still in   migration: drain,  │
         │  NetworkReady=False,  /etc/cni/net.d,     delete old         │
         │  cni plugin not       fighting Cilium     conflist +         │
         │  initialized          for IPs             cni0/flannel.1,    │
         │                                           install, uncordon  │
         │  Agent or operator    Stale or partial    Reapply cilium     │
         │  log shows a          ClusterRole --      install / helm     │
         │  CiliumNode           hand-edited         upgrade to         │
         │  "forbidden" RBAC     manifest or an      regenerate RBAC;   │
         │  error on create      interrupted Helm    verify with        │
         │                       upgrade             kubectl auth       │
         │                                           can-i              │
         │  cilium status stuck  Node below Ch5.1    Check free -h /    │
         │  at "Desired: 3,      minimums            nproc vs Ch5.1;    │
         │  Ready: 1" for        (RAM<4GB/CPU<2      kubectl describe   │
         │  several minutes      cores), or never    pod cilium-xxx     │
         │                       uncordoned          for                │
         │                       post-migration      Pending/OOMKilled  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    C.2  "Policy not working" troubleshooting
         - Ch9.3's Top-5-Mistakes table already covers label typos, missing

           method (DNS -> policy verdict -> routing -> application) rather than
           repeating it.
         - Full walkthroughs: Ch9.1 (the 4-step method), Ch9.2 (reading cilium

    C.3  Hubble connectivity issues
         - Two different failure classes live here: Hubble itself is broken
           (UI/Relay unreachable, observe returns nothing), or Hubble is working
           fine but the FLOW you want already scrolled out of its ring buffer.
         - Full walkthroughs: Ch13's Common Pitfalls (Relay/UI setup), and
           Ch15.5's flag quick-reference (--to-pod/--from-pod, --verdict, -o json).

    C.5  Encryption configuration errors
